Not to be confused with [Philippin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippin).

"Philippine" redirects here. For the town in the Netherlands, see [Philippine, Netherlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine,_Netherlands).

The **Philippines**,[^cite_note-21] officially the **Republic of the Philippines**,[^cite_note-22] is an [archipelagic country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archipelagic_state) in [Southeast Asia](28741.md). Located in the western [Pacific Ocean](23070.md), it consists of about [7,641 islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_islands_of_the_Philippines), with a total area of about 300,000 square kilometers. The islands are broadly categorized in [three main geographical divisions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Island_groups_of_the_Philippines). From north to south, the divisions are: [Luzon](69375.md), [Visayas](72418.md), and [Mindanao](20546.md). With a population of over 114 million, it is the world's [thirteenth-most-populous country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_and_dependencies_by_population).&#x20;

The Philippines is bounded by the [South China Sea](74209.md) to the west, the [Philippine Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine_Sea) to the east, and the [Celebes Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celebes_Sea) to the south. It shares [maritime borders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maritime_border) with [Taiwan](25734.md) to the north, [Japan](15573.md) to the northeast, [Palau](23372.md) to southeast, [Indonesia](14579.md) to the south, [Malaysia](3607937.md) to the southwest, [Vietnam](202354.md) to the west, and [China](5405.md) to the northwest. It has [diverse ethnicities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_groups_in_the_Philippines) and [a rich culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_the_Philippines). [Manila](184334.md) is [the country's capital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_of_the_Philippines), and [its most populated city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cities_of_the_Philippines#Largest_cities) is [Quezon City](284223.md). Both are within [Metro Manila](72340.md).&#x20;

[Negritos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Negrito), the archipelago's earliest inhabitants, were followed by [waves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Models_of_migration_to_the_Philippines) of [Austronesian peoples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austronesian_peoples). The adoption of [animism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Animism), [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism) with [Buddhist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhist) influence, and [Islam](6037917.md) established [island-kingdoms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Philippines_\(900%E2%80%931565\)). Extensive overseas trade with neighbors such as the late [Tang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tang_dynasty) or [Song](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Song) empire brought [Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sangley) people to the archipelago as well, who would also gradually settle in and [intermix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interethnic_marriage) over the centuries. The arrival of the explorer [Ferdinand Magellan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferdinand_Magellan) marked the beginning of [Spanish colonization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_Colonization_in_the_Philippines). In 1543, Spanish explorer [Ruy López de Villalobos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruy_L%C3%B3pez_de_Villalobos) named the archipelago *las Islas Filipinas* in honor of [King Philip II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philip_II_of_Spain). [Catholicism](606848.md) became the dominant religion, and Manila became the western hub of [trans-Pacific trade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_treasure_fleet). [Hispanic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spaniard) immigrants from [Latin America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_American_Asian) and [Iberia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iberia) would also selectively colonize. The [Philippine Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine_Revolution) began in 1896, and became entwined with the 1898 [Spanish–American War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish%E2%80%93American_War). Spain ceded the territory to the United States, and [Filipino revolutionaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_Junta) declared the [First Philippine Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Philippine_Republic). The ensuing [Philippine–American War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine%E2%80%93American_War) ended with the United States controlling the territory until the [Japanese invasion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippines_campaign_\(1941%E2%80%931942\)) of the islands during [World War II](32927.md). After [the United States retook the Philippines from the Japanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippines_campaign_\(1944%E2%80%931945\)), the Philippines became independent in 1946. Since then, the country notably experienced a [period of martial law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martial_law_under_Ferdinand_Marcos) from 1972 to 1981 under the dictatorship of [Ferdinand Marcos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferdinand_Marcos) and his subsequent overthrow by the [People Power Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People_Power_Revolution) in 1986, returning to democracy.&#x20;

The Philippines is an [emerging market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emerging_market) and a [developing](78449.md) and [newly industrialized country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newly_industrialized_country), [whose economy](23445.md) is transitioning from being agricultural to service- and manufacturing-centered. It has a variety of natural resources and a globally-significant [level of biodiversity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megadiverse_countries). The country is also part of [multiple international organizations and forums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_organization_membership_of_the_Philippines), mainly in the [Asia-Pacific](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asia-Pacific) region. Despite its fast economic growth, it continues to struggle with [inequality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Income_inequality_in_the_Philippines), widespread [corruption](28460051.md), and vulnerability to natural disasters due to its location both within the Pacific [Ring of Fire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ring_of_Fire) and near the [equator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Equator), making it prone to [earthquakes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_earthquakes_in_the_Philippines), [monsoon rains](57630.md), and [typhoons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Typhoons_in_the_Philippines), to which the Philippines has built a [resilience](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Community_resilience).&#x20;

## Etymology

Main article: [Names of the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Names_of_the_Philippines)

During his 1542 expedition, Spanish explorer [Ruy López de Villalobos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruy_L%C3%B3pez_de_Villalobos) named the islands of [Leyte](194750.md) and [Samar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samar) "*Felipinas*" after the [Prince of Asturias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_of_Asturias), later [Philip II of Castile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philip_II_of_Castile). Eventually, the name "*Las Islas Filipinas*" would be used for the archipelago's Spanish possessions.[^cite_note-Scott-1994-23]<sup>: [6](https://books.google.com/books?id=15KZU-yMuisC\&pg=PA6) </sup> Other names, such as "*Islas del Poniente*" (Western Islands), "*Islas del Oriente*" (Eastern Islands), Ferdinand Magellan's name, and "*San Lázaro*" (Islands of St. Lazarus), were used by the Spanish to refer to islands in the region before Spanish rule was established.[^cite_note-24][^cite_note-25][^cite_note-26]&#x20;

During the [Philippine Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine_Revolution), the [Malolos Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malolos_Congress) proclaimed it the *República Filipina* ([Philippine Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Philippine_Republic)) in 1899.[^cite_note-27] American colonial authorities referred to the country as the Philippine Islands (a translation of the Spanish name).[^cite_note-28] The [United States](3434750.md) began changing its nomenclature from "the Philippine Islands" to "the Philippines" in the Philippine Autonomy Act and the [Jones Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jones_Law_\(Philippines\)).[^cite_note-29] The official title "Republic of the Philippines" was included in the 1935 constitution as the name of the future independent state,[^cite_note-30] and in all succeeding constitutional revisions.[^cite_note-31][^cite_note-32]&#x20;

## History

Main article: [History of the Philippines](23441.md)

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of Philippine history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_Philippine_history).

### Prehistory (pre–900)

Main article: [Prehistory of the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prehistory_of_the_Philippines)

A burial jar with its lid decorated with two people on a boat

The [Manunggul burial jar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manunggul_Jar), one of the numerous [burial jars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burial_jar) found on the cave system

There is [evidence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archaeology_of_the_Philippines) of early [hominins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hominins) living in what is now the Philippines as early as 709,000 years ago.[^cite_note-33] Bones from [Callao Cave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Callao_Cave) potentially represent an otherwise unknown species, *[Homo luzonensis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homo_luzonensis)*, who lived 50,000 to 67,000 years ago.[^cite_note-34][^cite_note-35] The oldest [modern human](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_human) remains on the islands are from the [Tabon Caves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tabon_Caves) of [Palawan](240395.md), [U/Th-dated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U/Th-dated) to 47,000 ± 11–10,000 years ago.[^cite_note-36] [Tabon Man](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tabon_Man) is presumably a [Negrito](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Negrito), among the archipelago's earliest inhabitants descended from the first human migrations out of Africa via the coastal route along [southern Asia](21566765.md) to the now-sunken landmasses of [Sundaland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sundaland) and [Sahul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sahul).[^cite_note-37]&#x20;

The first Austronesians reached the Philippines from Taiwan around 2200 BC, settling the [Batanes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Batanes) Islands (where they built stone fortresses known as *[ijangs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ijang)*)[^cite_note-38] and northern [Luzon](69375.md). Jade artifacts have been dated to 2000 BC,[^cite_note-39][^cite_note-40] with [lingling-o](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lingling-o) jade items made in Luzon with raw materials from Taiwan.[^cite_note-41] By 1000 BC, the inhabitants of the archipelago had developed into four societies: [hunter-gatherer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunter-gatherer) tribes, warrior societies, highland [plutocracies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plutocracies), and port principalities.[^cite_note-Legarda-2001-42] The Philippines prior to foreign colonization was believed to be egalitarian.[^cite_note-43]&#x20;

### Early states (900–1565)

Main article: [History of the Philippines (900–1565)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Philippines_\(900%E2%80%931565\))

The earliest known surviving written record in the Philippines is the 900 AD [Laguna Copperplate Inscription](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laguna_Copperplate_Inscription), which was written in [Old Malay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Malay) using the early [Kawi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kawi_alphabet) script.[^cite_note-44] By the 14th century, several large coastal settlements emerged as trading centers and became the focus of [societal changes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_achievements_of_pre-colonial_Philippines).[^cite_note-deGraaf-1977-45] Some [polities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polities) had exchanges with other states throughout Asia.[^cite_note-Junker-1999-46]<sup>: 3 </sup>[^cite_note-47] Trade with China began during the late [Tang dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tang_dynasty),[^cite_note-48][^cite_note-\:0-49] and expanded during the [Song dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Song_dynasty).[^cite_note-50][^cite_note-51][^cite_note-\:0-49] Throughout the second millennium AD, some polities were part of the [tributary system of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tributary_system_of_China).[^cite_note-Scott-1994-23]<sup>: 177–178 </sup>[^cite_note-Junker-1999-46]<sup>: 3 </sup> With extensive trade and diplomacy, this brought [Southern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_and_southern_China) [Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Han_Chinese) merchants and migrants from [Southern Fujian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Fujian),[^cite_note-52][^cite_note-53][^cite_note-54] who would gradually settle and intermix in the Philippines. Indian cultural traits such as linguistic terms and religious practices [began to spread](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_influences_in_early_Philippine_polities) in the Philippines during the 14th century, via the Indianized Hindu [Majapahit Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Majapahit).[^cite_note-55][^cite_note-56] By the 15th century, Islam was established in the [Sulu Archipelago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sulu_Archipelago) and spread from there.[^cite_note-deGraaf-1977-45]



A couple portrayed in 1590's Early Spanish colonial period of the Philippines, draped in gold

Polities founded in the Philippines between the 10th and 16th centuries include [Maynila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maynila_\(historical_polity\)),[^cite_note-Ring-1996-57] [Tondo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tondo_\(historical_polity\)), [Namayan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Namayan), [Pangasinan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pangasinan_\(historical_polity\)), [Caboloan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caboloan), [Cebu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cebu_\(historical_state\)), [Butuan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butuan_\(historical_polity\)), [Maguindanao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sultanate_of_Maguindanao), [Lanao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confederate_States_of_Lanao), [Sulu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sultanate_of_Sulu), and [Ma-i](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ma-i).[^cite_note-58] The early polities typically had a three-tier social structure: nobility, freemen, and dependent debtor-bondsmen.[^cite_note-Junker-1999-46]<sup>: 3 </sup>[^cite_note-Wernstedt-1967-59]<sup>: [672](https://books.google.com/books?id=6Pn0Pfh1Cl0C\&pg=PA672) </sup> Among the nobility were leaders known as [datus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Datu), who were responsible for ruling autonomous groups ([barangays](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Precolonial_barangay) or dulohan).[^cite_note-60] When the barangays banded together to form a larger settlement or a geographically looser alliance,[^cite_note-Junker-1999-46]<sup>: 3 </sup>[^cite_note-61] their more-esteemed members would be recognized as a "[paramount datu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount_rulers_in_early_Philippine_history)",[^cite_note-Newson-62]<sup>: [58](https://books.google.com/books?id=A40BEAAAQBAJ\&pg=PA58) </sup>[^cite_note-Legarda-2001-42] [rajah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rajah) or [sultan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sultan),[^cite_note-63] and would rule the community.[^cite_note-64] Population density is thought to have been low during the 14th to 16th centuries[^cite_note-Newson-62]<sup>: [18](https://books.google.com/books?id=A40BEAAAQBAJ\&pg=PA18) </sup> due to the [frequency of typhoons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Typhoon#Frequency) and the Philippines' location on the Pacific [Ring of Fire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ring_of_Fire).[^cite_note-65] Portuguese explorer [Ferdinand Magellan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferdinand_Magellan) arrived in 1521, claimed the islands for Spain, and was killed by [Lapulapu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lapulapu)'s men in the [Battle of Mactan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Mactan).[^cite_note-Woods-2006-66]<sup>: [21](https://books.google.com/books?id=2Z-n_kDTxf0C\&pg=PT46) </sup>[^cite_note-Guillermo-2012-67]<sup>: [261](https://books.google.com/books?id=wmgX9M_yETIC\&pg=PA261) </sup>&#x20;

### Spanish and American colonial rule (1565–1934)

Main articles: [History of the Philippines (1565–1898)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Philippines_\(1565%E2%80%931898\)) and [History of the Philippines (1898–1946)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Philippines_\(1898%E2%80%931946\))

See caption

[Manila](184334.md), 1847

Unification and colonization by the [Crown of Castile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crown_of_Castile) began when Spanish explorer [Miguel López de Legazpi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miguel_L%C3%B3pez_de_Legazpi) arrived from [New Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Spain) in 1565.[^cite_note-68][^cite_note-69][^cite_note-deBorja-2005-70]<sup>: 20–23 </sup> Many [Filipinos](2298105.md) were brought to New Spain [as slaves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Spanish_slavery_in_the_Philippines) and forced crew,[^cite_note-71] whereas many [Latin Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_American_Asian) were brought to the Philippines as soldiers and colonists.[^cite_note-72] The Philippines hosts the only Latin American established districts in Asia.[^cite_note-73][^cite_note-74] [Spanish Manila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intramuros) became the capital of the [Captaincy General of the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Captaincy_General_of_the_Philippines) and the [Spanish East Indies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_East_Indies) in 1571,[^cite_note-75][^cite_note-76] the Spanish territories in Asia and the Pacific.[^cite_note-77] The Spanish invaded local states using the principle of [divide and conquer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Divide_and_rule),[^cite_note-Guillermo-2012-67]<sup>: [374](https://books.google.com/books?id=wmgX9M_yETIC\&pg=PA374) </sup> bringing most of what is the present-day Philippines under one unified administration.[^cite_note-78][^cite_note-79]&#x20;

Disparate barangays were deliberately [consolidated into towns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reductions), where [Catholic missionaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friars_in_Spanish_Philippines) could more easily convert their inhabitants to [Christianity](5211.md),[^cite_note-Abinales-2005-80]<sup>: [53](https://books.google.com/books?id=xiOQdEzgP9kC\&pg=PA53), [68](https://books.google.com/books?id=xiOQdEzgP9kC\&pg=PA68) </sup>[^cite_note-81] which was initially [syncretist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religious_Syncretism).[^cite_note-82] [Christianization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianization) by the Spanish friars occurred mostly across the settled lowlands over the course of time. From 1565 to 1821, the Philippines was governed as a territory of the [Mexico City](18987.md)-based Viceroyalty of New Spain; it was then administered from [Madrid](41188263.md) after the [Mexican War of Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_War_of_Independence).[^cite_note-Halili-2004-83]<sup>: [81](https://books.google.com/books?id=gUt5v8ET4QYC\&pg=PA81) </sup> Manila became the western hub of [trans-Pacific trade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_treasure_fleet)[^cite_note-84] by [Manila galleons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manila_galleon) built in [Bicol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bicol_Region) and [Cavite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cavite).[^cite_note-85][^cite_note-86]&#x20;

During its rule, Spain spent a significant sum of its treasury quelling [indigenous revolts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine_revolts_against_Spain)[^cite_note-Halili-2004-83]<sup>: [111–122](https://books.google.com/books?id=gUt5v8ET4QYC\&pg=PA111) </sup> and defending against external military attacks,[^cite_note-Ooi-2004-87]<sup>: [1077](https://books.google.com/books?id=QKgraWbb7yoC\&pg=PA1077) </sup>[^cite_note-88] including [Moro piracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piracy_in_the_Sulu_and_Celebes_Seas),[^cite_note-89] a 17th-century [war against the Dutch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battles_of_La_Naval_de_Manila), 18th-century [British occupation of Manila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_occupation_of_Manila), and conflict with Muslims in the south.[^cite_note-Dolan-1991-90]<sup>: [4](https://web.archive.org/web/20060927161256/http://countrystudies.us/philippines/4.htm) </sup>&#x20;

Administration of the Philippines was considered a drain on the economy of New Spain,[^cite_note-Ooi-2004-87]<sup>: [1077](https://books.google.com/books?id=QKgraWbb7yoC\&pg=PA1077) </sup> and abandoning it or trading it for other territory was debated. This course of action was opposed because of the islands' economic potential, security, and the desire to continue religious conversion in the region.[^cite_note-Newson-62]<sup>: [7–8](https://books.google.com/books?id=A40BEAAAQBAJ\&pg=PA7) </sup>[^cite_note-91] The colony survived on an annual subsidy from the Spanish crown[^cite_note-Ooi-2004-87]<sup>: [1077](https://books.google.com/books?id=QKgraWbb7yoC\&pg=PA1077) </sup> averaging 250,000 pesos,[^cite_note-Newson-62]<sup>: [8](https://books.google.com/books?id=A40BEAAAQBAJ\&pg=PA8) </sup> usually paid as 75 tons of silver bullion from the Americas.[^cite_note-92] [British forces occupied Manila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_occupation_of_Manila) from 1762 to 1764 during the [Seven Years' War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seven_Years%27_War). Spanish rule was restored with the [1763 Treaty of Paris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Paris_\(1763\)).[^cite_note-deBorja-2005-70]<sup>: 81–83 </sup> The Spanish considered their war with the Muslims in Southeast Asia an extension of the *[Reconquista](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reconquista)*.[^cite_note-93][^cite_note-94] The [Spanish–Moro conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish%E2%80%93Moro_conflict) lasted for several hundred years. Spain conquered portions of [Mindanao](20546.md) and [Jolo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jolo) during the last quarter of the 19th century,[^cite_note-95] and the Muslim [Moro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moro_people) in the [Sultanate of Sulu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sultanate_of_Sulu) acknowledged Spanish sovereignty.[^cite_note-96][^cite_note-97]&#x20;

Photo of a large group of men on steps. Some are seated, and others are standing; several are wearing top hats.

*[Ilustrados](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilustrado)* in [Madrid](41188263.md) around 1890

Philippine ports opened to world trade during the 19th century, and Filipino society began to change.[^cite_note-98][^cite_note-99] Social identity changed, with the term *Filipino* encompassing all residents of the archipelago instead of solely referring to [Spaniards born in the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_Filipino).[^cite_note-100][^cite_note-101]&#x20;

Revolutionary sentiment grew in 1872 after 200 locally recruited [colonial troops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colonial_troops) and laborers alongside [three activist Catholic priests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gomburza) were executed on [questionable grounds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1872_Cavite_mutiny).[^cite_note-102][^cite_note-103] This inspired the [Propaganda Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Propaganda_Movement), organized by [Marcelo H. del Pilar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcelo_H._del_Pilar), [José Rizal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Rizal), [Graciano López Jaena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graciano_L%C3%B3pez_Jaena), and [Mariano Ponce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariano_Ponce), which advocated political reform in the Philippines.[^cite_note-104] Rizal was executed on December 30, 1896, for rebellion, and his death radicalized many who had been loyal to Spain.[^cite_note-105] Attempts at reform met with resistance; [Andrés Bonifacio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andr%C3%A9s_Bonifacio) founded the [Katipunan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katipunan) secret society, which sought independence from Spain through armed revolt, in 1892.[^cite_note-Halili-2004-83]<sup>: [137](https://books.google.com/books?id=gUt5v8ET4QYC\&pg=PA137) </sup>&#x20;

The Katipunan [Cry of Pugad Lawin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cry_of_Pugad_Lawin) began the [Philippine Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine_Revolution) in 1896.[^cite_note-106] Internal disputes led to the [Tejeros Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tejeros_Convention), at which Bonifacio lost his position and [Emilio Aguinaldo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emilio_Aguinaldo) was elected the new leader of the revolution.[^cite_note-Duka-2008-107]<sup>: [145–147](https://books.google.com/books?id=4wk8yqCEmJUC\&pg=PA147) </sup> The 1897 [Pact of Biak-na-Bato](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pact_of_Biak-na-Bato) resulted in the [Hong Kong Junta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_Junta) government in exile. The [Spanish–American War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish%E2%80%93American_War) began the following year, and reached the Philippines; Aguinaldo returned, resumed the revolution, and [declared independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine_Declaration_of_Independence) from Spain on June 12, 1898.[^cite_note-Abinales-2022-108]<sup>: [26](https://books.google.com/books?id=0Hd3EAAAQBAJ\&pg=PA26) </sup> In December 1898, the islands were [ceded by Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Paris_\(1898\)) to the United States with [Puerto Rico](23041.md) and [Guam](11974.md) after the [Spanish–American War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish%E2%80%93American_War).[^cite_note-109][^cite_note-110] [Spain](26667.md) had ruled the Philippines for 333 years.[^cite_note-111]&#x20;

The [First Philippine Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Philippine_Republic) was promulgated on January 21, 1899.[^cite_note-112] Lack of recognition by the United States led to an [outbreak of hostilities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Manila_\(1899\)) that, after refusal by the U.S. on-scene military commander of a cease-fire proposal and a declaration of war by the nascent Republic,[^cite_note-113] escalated into the [Philippine–American War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine%E2%80%93American_War).[^cite_note-Nation18990504-114][^cite_note-115][^cite_note-116][^cite_note-117]&#x20;



Filipino General [Gregorio del Pilar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gregorio_del_Pilar) and his troops in Pampanga around 1898, during the [Philippine-American War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine-American_War)

The war resulted in the deaths of 250,000 to 1 million civilians, primarily due to famine and disease.[^cite_note-Tucker-2009-118] Many Filipinos were transported by the Americans to [concentration camps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_concentration_and_internment_camps#Philippines), where thousands died.[^cite_note-119][^cite_note-120] After the fall of the First Philippine Republic in 1902, an [American civilian government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Insular_Government_of_the_Philippine_Islands) was established with the [Philippine Organic Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine_Organic_Act_\(1902\)).[^cite_note-121] American forces continued to secure and extend their control of the islands, suppressing an attempted [extension of the Philippine Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tagalog_Republic#Sakay),[^cite_note-Duka-2008-107]<sup>: [200–202](https://books.google.com/books?id=4wk8yqCEmJUC\&pg=PA200) </sup>[^cite_note-Tucker-2009-118] [securing the Sultanate of Sulu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiram%E2%80%93Bates_Treaty),[^cite_note-122][^cite_note-123] establishing control of interior mountainous areas which had resisted Spanish conquest,[^cite_note-124] and encouraging large-scale resettlement of Christians in once-predominantly-Muslim Mindanao.[^cite_note-125][^cite_note-126]&#x20;

Rather than actively resisting or being transformed by American colonial rule, Filipino elites neutralized intended political reforms by "domesticating" US tutelary institutions through their own preexisting cultural schemas, thereby reproducing traditional power dynamics.[^cite_note-127]&#x20;

### Commonwealth and World War II (1935–1946)

Further information: [Commonwealth of the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_of_the_Philippines) and [Military history of the Philippines during World War II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_history_of_the_Philippines_during_World_War_II)

Cultural developments in the Philippines strengthened a national identity,[^cite_note-Armes-1987-128][^cite_note-Tofighian-2006-129]<sup>: 12 </sup> and [Tagalog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tagalog_language) began to take precedence over other local languages.[^cite_note-Abinales-2005-80]<sup>: [121](https://books.google.com/books?id=xiOQdEzgP9kC\&pg=PA121) </sup> Governmental functions were gradually given to Filipinos by the [Taft Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taft_Commission);[^cite_note-Ooi-2004-87]<sup>: [1081](https://books.google.com/books?id=QKgraWbb7yoC\&pg=PA1081), [1117](https://books.google.com/books?id=QKgraWbb7yoC\&pg=PA1117) </sup> the 1934 [Tydings–McDuffie Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tydings%E2%80%93McDuffie_Act) granted a ten-year transition to independence through the creation of the Commonwealth of the Philippines the following year,[^cite_note-Nadeau-2020-130] with [Manuel Quezon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manuel_L._Quezon) president and [Sergio Osmeña](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Osme%C3%B1a) vice president.[^cite_note-131] Quezon's priorities were defence, social justice, inequality, economic diversification, and national character.[^cite_note-Ooi-2004-87]<sup>: [1081](https://books.google.com/books?id=QKgraWbb7yoC\&pg=PA1081), [1117](https://books.google.com/books?id=QKgraWbb7yoC\&pg=PA1117) </sup> [Filipino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filipino_language) (a standardized variety of Tagalog) became the national language,[^cite_note-Thompson-2003-132]<sup>: [27–29](https://books.google.com/books?id=W1h9oF9rj-MC\&pg=PA27) </sup> [women's suffrage was introduced](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1937_Philippine_women%27s_suffrage_plebiscite),[^cite_note-133][^cite_note-Guillermo-2012-67]<sup>: [416](https://books.google.com/books?id=wmgX9M_yETIC\&pg=PA416) </sup> and [land reform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land_reform_in_the_Philippines) was considered.[^cite_note-134][^cite_note-135][^cite_note-136] The introduction of women's suffrage signified a progressive step towards [gender equality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_equality) in the Philippines. It gave women the right to vote and participate in the country's political processes.&#x20;

Douglas MacArthur, Sergio Osmeña, and Osmeña's staff wading ashore in knee-deep water

[General Douglas MacArthur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Douglas_MacArthur) and [Sergio Osmeña](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Osme%C3%B1a) *(left)* coming ashore during the [Battle of Leyte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Leyte) on October 20, 1944

The [Empire of Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_of_Japan) invaded the Philippines in December 1941 [during World War II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_history_of_the_Philippines_during_World_War_II),[^cite_note-137] and the [Second Philippine Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Philippine_Republic) was established as a [puppet state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puppet_state) governed by [Jose P. Laurel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jose_P._Laurel).[^cite_note-138][^cite_note-139] Beginning in 1942, the [Japanese occupation of the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_occupation_of_the_Philippines) was [opposed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine_resistance_against_Japan) by large-scale [underground guerrilla activity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_occupation_of_the_Philippines#Resistance).[^cite_note-140][^cite_note-141][^cite_note-142] [Atrocities and war crimes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_war_crimes) were committed during the war, including the [Bataan Death March](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bataan_Death_March) and the [Manila massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manila_massacre).[^cite_note-143][^cite_note-144] The Philippine resistance and Allied troops [defeated the Japanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippines_campaign_\(1944%E2%80%931945\)) in 1944 and 1945. Over one million Filipinos were estimated to have died by the end of the war.[^cite_note-145][^cite_note-146] On October 11, 1945, the Philippines became a [founding member](31969.md) of the [United Nations](31769.md).[^cite_note-147][^cite_note-Buhler-2001-148]<sup>: [38–41](https://books.google.com/books?id=Ty7NAG1Jl-8C\&pg=PA38) </sup> On July 4, 1946, during the presidency of [Manuel Roxas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manuel_Roxas), the country's independence was recognized by the United States with the [Treaty of Manila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Manila_\(1946\)).[^cite_note-Buhler-2001-148]<sup>: [38–41](https://books.google.com/books?id=Ty7NAG1Jl-8C\&pg=PA38) </sup>[^cite_note-149]&#x20;

The Tydings-McDuffie Act was signed by U.S. President [Franklin D. Roosevelt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franklin_D._Roosevelt) on March 24, 1934 in the [Great Depression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Depression_in_the_United_States) period. When the act was signed, [Manuel L. Quezon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manuel_L._Quezon) became the 2nd President of the Philippines, which made the Philippines a Commonwealth after [Emilio Aguinaldo y Famy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emilio_Aguinaldo). President [Harry S. Truman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harry_S._Truman) signed Proclamation 2695 and Truman authorized [Paul V. McNutt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_V._McNutt) to proclaim the independence of the Philippines in the [Treaty of Manila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Manila_\(1946\)) on July 4, 1946 following the [Tydings-McDuffie Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tydings%E2%80%93McDuffie_Act).[^cite_note-150]&#x20;

### Independence (1946–present)

Main articles: [History of the Philippines (1946–1965)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Philippines_\(1946%E2%80%931965\)), [History of the Philippines (1965–1986)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Philippines_\(1965%E2%80%931986\)), and [History of the Philippines (1986–present)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Philippines_\(1986%E2%80%93present\))

Efforts at post-war reconstruction and ending the [Hukbalahap Rebellion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hukbalahap_Rebellion) succeeded during [Ramon Magsaysay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramon_Magsaysay)'s presidency,[^cite_note-151] but sporadic communist insurgency continued to flare up long afterward.[^cite_note-Tucker-2013-152] Under Magsaysay's successor, [Carlos P. Garcia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_P._Garcia), the government initiated a [Filipino First policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filipino_First_policy) which promoted Filipino-owned businesses.[^cite_note-Abinales-2005-80]<sup>: [182](https://books.google.com/books?id=xiOQdEzgP9kC\&pg=PA182) </sup> Succeeding Garcia, [Diosdado Macapagal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diosdado_Macapagal) moved Independence Day from July 4 to June 12—the date of Emilio Aguinaldo's declaration—[^cite_note-153] and pursued [a claim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Borneo_dispute) on eastern [North Borneo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Borneo).[^cite_note-154][^cite_note-155]&#x20;



The attending leaders of the [Manila Summit Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manila_Summit_Conference) in front of the Congress Building in Manila, hosted by [Ferdinand Marcos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferdinand_Marcos) on October 24, 1966

In 1965, Macapagal [lost](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1965_Philippine_presidential_election) the presidential election to [Ferdinand Marcos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferdinand_Marcos). [Early in his presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_term_of_the_presidency_of_Ferdinand_Marcos), Marcos began infrastructure projects funded mostly by foreign loans; this improved the economy, and contributed to his [reelection in 1969](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1969_Philippine_presidential_election).[^cite_note-Timberman-1991-156]<sup>: [58](https://books.google.com/books?id=NkBO2RhI4NUC\&pg=PA58) </sup>[^cite_note-157] Near the end of his last constitutionally-permitted term, Marcos [declared martial law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proclamation_No._1081) on September 21, 1972[^cite_note-158] using the specter of communism[^cite_note-159][^cite_note-160][^cite_note-161] and began to [rule by decree](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rule_by_decree);[^cite_note-TheEuropaWorldYear-2004-162] the period was characterized by [political repression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_detainees_under_the_Marcos_dictatorship), [censorship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Journalism_during_the_Marcos_dictatorship), and [human rights violations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_abuses_of_the_Marcos_dictatorship).[^cite_note-163][^cite_note-164] [Monopolies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monopolies_in_the_Philippines_\(1965%E2%80%931986\)) controlled by [Marcos's cronies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cronies_of_Ferdinand_Marcos) were established in key industries,[^cite_note-165][^cite_note-166][^cite_note-167] including [logging](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deforestation_in_the_Philippines#Deforestation_during_the_martial_law_era)[^cite_note-168] and broadcasting;[^cite_note-Guillermo-2012-67]<sup>: [120](https://books.google.com/books?id=wmgX9M_yETIC\&pg=PA120) </sup> a sugar monopoly led to [a famine on the island of Negros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Negros_famine).[^cite_note-169] With his wife, [Imelda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imelda_Marcos), Marcos was accused of corruption and [embezzling billions of dollars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unexplained_wealth_of_the_Marcos_family) of public funds.[^cite_note-170][^cite_note-171] Marcos's heavy borrowing [early in his presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1969_Philippine_balance_of_payments_crisis) resulted in [economic crashes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_history_of_the_Philippines_\(1965%E2%80%931986\)), exacerbated by an [early 1980s recession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Early_1980s_recession) where the economy contracted by 7.3 percent annually in 1984 and 1985.[^cite_note-Thompson-Batalla-2018-172]<sup>: [212](https://books.google.com/books?id=DmkPEAAAQBAJ\&pg=PA212) </sup>[^cite_note-173]&#x20;

On August 21, 1983, opposition leader [Benigno Aquino Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ninoy_Aquino) was [assassinated on the tarmac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_of_Ninoy_Aquino) at [Manila International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ninoy_Aquino_International_Airport).[^cite_note-174] Marcos called a snap [presidential election in 1986](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1986_Philippine_presidential_election)[^cite_note-175] which proclaimed him the winner, but the results were widely regarded as fraudulent.[^cite_note-176] The resulting protests led to the [People Power Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People_Power_Revolution),[^cite_note-LATimes-3DayRevolution-177][^cite_note-178] which forced Marcos and his allies to flee to [Hawaii](13270.md). Aquino's widow, [Corazon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corazon_Aquino), was installed as president[^cite_note-LATimes-3DayRevolution-177] and a new constitution was promulgated.[^cite_note-179]&#x20;

The return of democracy and government reforms which began in 1986 were hampered by [national debt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_debt_of_the_Philippines), government corruption, and [coup attempts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coup_attempts_against_Corazon_Aquino).[^cite_note-180][^cite_note-Timberman-1991-156]<sup>: xii, xiii </sup> A [communist insurgency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_rebellion_in_the_Philippines)[^cite_note-181][^cite_note-182] and military conflict with [Moro separatists](4602307.md) persisted;[^cite_note-183] the administration also faced a series of disasters, including the eruption of [Mount Pinatubo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Pinatubo) in June 1991.[^cite_note-usgs-184] Aquino was succeeded by [Fidel V. Ramos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fidel_V._Ramos), who [liberalized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_liberalization) the national economy with [privatization](24661.md) and [deregulation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deregulation).[^cite_note-185][^cite_note-Ortega-NeoliberalizingSpaces-2016-186] Ramos's economic gains were overshadowed by the onset of the [1997 Asian financial crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1997_Asian_financial_crisis).[^cite_note-NYTimes-Gargan-1997-187][^cite_note-188] His successor, [Joseph Estrada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Estrada), prioritized public housing[^cite_note-189] but faced corruption allegations[^cite_note-190] which led to his overthrow by the [2001 EDSA Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_EDSA_Revolution) and the succession of Vice President [Gloria Macapagal Arroyo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gloria_Macapagal_Arroyo) on January 20, 2001.[^cite_note-191]&#x20;

Arroyo's [nine-year administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Gloria_Macapagal_Arroyo) was marked by economic growth,[^cite_note-CIAWorldFactBook-15] but was tainted by corruption and political scandals,[^cite_note-192][^cite_note-193] including [electoral fraud allegations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hello_Garci_scandal) during the [2004 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_Philippine_presidential_election).[^cite_note-194] Economic growth continued during [Benigno Aquino III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benigno_Aquino_III)'s administration, which advocated good governance and transparency.[^cite_note-Lum-Dolven-2014-195]<sup>: 1, 3 </sup>[^cite_note-196] Aquino III signed [a peace agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comprehensive_Agreement_on_the_Bangsamoro) with the [Moro Islamic Liberation Front](51673.md) (MILF) resulting in the [Bangsamoro Organic Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangsamoro_Organic_Law) establishing an autonomous [Bangsamoro](37260549.md) region, but a [shootout with MILF rebels in Mamasapano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mamasapano_clash) delayed passage of the law.[^cite_note-197][^cite_note-198]&#x20;

Growing public frustration with post-[EDSA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People_Power_Revolution) governance led to the [2016 election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Philippine_presidential_election)[^cite_note-199] of populist [Rodrigo Duterte](7407293.md),[^cite_note-CasipleEtAl-2016-200][^cite_note-201] whose [presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Rodrigo_Duterte) saw the decline of [liberalism in the country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberalism_in_the_Philippines) albeit largely retaining liberal economic policies.[^cite_note-202][^cite_note-Beauchamp-2022-203] Among Duterte's priorities was [aggressively increasing infrastructure spending](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Build!_Build!_Build!) to spur economic growth;[^cite_note-204][^cite_note-205] the enactment of the Bangsamoro Organic Law;[^cite_note-206] an intensified crackdown on crime and communist insurgencies;[^cite_note-207] and [an anti-drug campaign](51032591.md) that reduced drug proliferation[^cite_note-208] but that has also led to [extrajudicial killings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extrajudicial_killing).[^cite_note-209][^cite_note-210] In early 2020, the [COVID-19 pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_the_Philippines) reached the Philippines,[^cite_note-211][^cite_note-212] necessitating [nationwide lockdowns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_community_quarantines_in_the_Philippines) that caused a brief but severe [economic recession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_recession).[^cite_note-213][^cite_note-214] Under a promise of continuing Duterte's policies,[^cite_note-Beauchamp-2022-203] Marcos's son, [Bongbong Marcos](572460.md), ran with Duterte's daughter, [Sara](42504861.md), and won the [2022 election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Philippine_presidential_election).[^cite_note-215] Marcos's renewal of a pro-US foreign policy, however, has been viewed as a reversal of Duterte's cordiality with China, and [territorial disputes in the South China Sea](32005536.md) have since escalated.[^cite_note-216] The Marcos administration also [arrested](79430656.md) his predecessor Duterte, who was sent to the [International Criminal Court](14880.md) in the [Netherlands](21148.md) for trial of [crimes against humanity](61099.md), which led to the collapse of the Marcos-Duterte alliance.[^cite_note-217] Later on, a series of [corruption, mismanagement and irregularities in government-funded flood management projects](80804811.md) occurred under his administration, resulting in [numerous protests nationwide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/September_2025_Philippine_protests).[^cite_note-218] The country suffered an [energy crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_energy_crisis_in_the_Philippines) in 2026 as a result of the [conflict in Middle East](82533582.md).[^cite_note-219] The first [parliamentary election](68697708.md) in the Bangsamoro region was held on September 14, 2026.[^cite_note-220]&#x20;

## Geography

Main article: [Geography of the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_the_Philippines)

Further information: [List of islands of the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_islands_of_the_Philippines)

Map of the Philippines, color-coded by elevation

The Philippines is generally mountainous. Uplands make up 65 percent of the Philippines's land area.

[^cite_note-Wernstedt-1967-59]

: [38](https://books.google.com/books?id=6Pn0Pfh1Cl0C\&pg=PA38)&#x20;

[^cite_note-221]

The Philippines is an archipelago of approximately [7,641 islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_islands_of_the_Philippines),[^cite_note-222][^cite_note-223] covering a total area (including inland bodies of water) of about 300,000 square kilometers (115,831 sq mi).[^cite_note-NAMRIAGovPH-InfoMapper-1991-224][^cite_note-Boquet-2017-225]<sup>: 15 </sup>[^cite_note-CIAWorldFactBook-15][^cite_note-land-area-14] Stretching 1,850 kilometers (1,150 mi) north to south,[^cite_note-227] from the [South China Sea](74209.md) to the [Celebes Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celebes_Sea),[^cite_note-228] the Philippines [is bordered](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borders_of_the_Philippines) by the [Philippine Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine_Sea) to the east,[^cite_note-229][^cite_note-230] and the [Sulu Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sulu_Sea) to the southwest.[^cite_note-231] The [Luzon Strait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luzon_Strait) is the most economical (quickest) traffic node for shipping between the [Americas](29833.md) and [Asia](689.md).[^cite_note-232][^cite_note-233][^cite_note-234] It is also a critical [junction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electrical_junction) of Asia-Pacific-Americas [undersea communication cables](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Undersea_communication_cable).[^cite_note-235][^cite_note-236][^cite_note-237][^cite_note-238] The [country's 11 largest islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_islands_of_the_Philippines) are [Luzon](69375.md), [Mindanao](20546.md), [Samar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samar), [Negros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Negros), [Palawan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palawan_\(island\)), [Panay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panay), [Mindoro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mindoro), [Leyte](194750.md), [Cebu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cebu_\(island\)), [Bohol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bohol) and [Masbate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masbate_\(island\)), about 95 percent of its total land area.[^cite_note-239] The Philippines' coastline measures 36,289 kilometers (22,549 mi), the world's [fifth-longest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_length_of_coastline),[^cite_note-240] and the [country's exclusive economic zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exclusive_economic_zone_of_the_Philippines) covers 2,263,816 km<sup>2</sup> (874,064 sq mi).[^cite_note-241]&#x20;

Its [highest mountain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_mountains_in_the_Philippines) is [Mount Apo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Apo) on Mindanao, with an altitude of 2,954 meters (9,692 ft) above sea level.[^cite_note-CIAWorldFactBook-15] The Philippines' [longest river](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_rivers_of_the_Philippines) is the [Cagayan River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cagayan_River) in northern Luzon, which flows for about 520 kilometers (320 mi).[^cite_note-242] [Manila Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manila_Bay), on which is the capital city of [Manila](184334.md),[^cite_note-243] is connected to [Laguna de Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laguna_de_Bay)[^cite_note-244] (the country's [largest lake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_lakes_of_the_Philippines)) by the [Pasig River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pasig_River).[^cite_note-245]&#x20;

On the western fringes of the Pacific [Ring of Fire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ring_of_Fire), the Philippines has frequent seismic and volcanic activity.[^cite_note-Rodell-2002-246]<sup>: [4](https://books.google.com/books?id=y1CVR74_KHQC\&pg=PA4) </sup> The region is [seismically](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seismology) active, and has been [constructed by plates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subduction_tectonics_of_the_Philippines) converging towards each other from multiple directions.[^cite_note-247][^cite_note-248] About five earthquakes are recorded daily, although most are too weak to be felt.[^cite_note-249] The [last major earthquakes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_earthquakes_in_the_Philippines) were in 1976 in the [Moro Gulf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1976_Moro_Gulf_earthquake) and in 1990 on [Luzon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1990_Luzon_earthquake).[^cite_note-250] The Philippines has [23 active volcanoes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_active_volcanoes_in_the_Philippines); of them, [Mayon](256485.md), [Taal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taal_Volcano), [Canlaon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Canlaon), and [Bulusan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Bulusan) have the largest number of recorded eruptions.[^cite_note-251][^cite_note-Boquet-2017-225]<sup>: [26](https://books.google.com/books?id=90C4DgAAQBAJ\&pg=PA26) </sup>&#x20;

The country has valuable[^cite_note-252] mineral deposits as a result of its complex geologic structure and high level of seismic activity.[^cite_note-253][^cite_note-254] It is thought to have the world's second-largest gold deposits (after [South Africa](17416221.md)), large copper deposits,[^cite_note-NYTimes-Miners-255] and the world's largest deposits of [palladium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palladium).[^cite_note-Inquirer-FirmSeesMetal-256] The country's gold production in 2015 is 21 metric tonnes.[^cite_note-257] Other minerals include [chromium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chromium), [nickel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nickel), [molybdenum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Molybdenum), [platinum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Platinum), and [zinc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zinc).[^cite_note-258] However, poor management and law enforcement, opposition from indigenous communities, and past environmental damage have left these resources largely untapped.[^cite_note-NYTimes-Miners-255][^cite_note-259]&#x20;

### Biodiversity

Main article: [Wildlife of the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wildlife_of_the_Philippines)

See also: [List of threatened species of the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_threatened_species_of_the_Philippines) and [Ecoregions in the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecoregions_in_the_Philippines)

Water buffalo with large, curved horns, seen from above

The [carabao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carabao) is the national animal of the Philippines. It symbolizes strength, power, efficiency, perseverance and hard work.

[^cite_note-260]

The Philippines is a [megadiverse country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megadiverse_countries),[^cite_note-Berba-Matias-2022-261][^cite_note-262] with some of the world's highest rates of discovery and [endemism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ecoregions_with_high_endemism) (67 percent).[^cite_note-OECD-2017April-263][^cite_note-264] With an [estimated 13,500 plant species](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flora_of_the_Philippines) in the country (3,500 of which are endemic),[^cite_note-265] Philippine rain forests have an array of flora:[^cite_note-266][^cite_note-267] about 3,500 species of trees,[^cite_note-268] 8,000 [flowering plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flowering_plant) species, 1,100 [ferns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fern), and 998 [orchid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_the_orchids_of_the_Philippines) species[^cite_note-269] have been identified.[^cite_note-Sajise-2010-270] The Philippines has 167 terrestrial [mammals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mammal) (102 endemic species), 235 [reptiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reptile) (160 endemic species), 99 [amphibians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amphibian) (74 endemic species), 686 [birds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_birds_of_the_Philippines) (224 endemic species),[^cite_note-271] and over 20,000 [insect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Insect) species.[^cite_note-Sajise-2010-270]&#x20;

As an important part of the [Coral Triangle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coral_Triangle) ecoregion,[^cite_note-272][^cite_note-273] Philippine waters have unique, diverse marine life[^cite_note-CalAcademyOrg-2015-274] and the world's greatest diversity of shore-fish species.[^cite_note-275] The country has over 3,200 fish species, 121 endemic.[^cite_note-276] Philippine waters sustain [the cultivation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aquaculture_in_the_Philippines) of fish, crustaceans, oysters, and seaweeds.[^cite_note-277][^cite_note-278]&#x20;

Eight major types of forests are distributed throughout the Philippines: [dipterocarp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dipterocarpaceae), [beach](40630.md) forest,[^cite_note-279] [pine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pine) forest, [molave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vitex_parviflora) forest, [lower montane forest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montane_ecosystems), upper montane (or [mossy forest](58903.md)), [mangroves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mangrove), and [ultrabasic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ultramafic_rock) forest.[^cite_note-280] According to official estimates, the Philippines had 7,000,000 hectares (27,000 sq mi) of forest cover in 2023.[^cite_note-281] Logging had been systemized during the American colonial period[^cite_note-282] and [deforestation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deforestation_in_the_Philippines) continued after independence, accelerating during the [Marcos presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martial_law_under_Ferdinand_Marcos) due to unregulated logging concessions.[^cite_note-283][^cite_note-284] Forest cover declined from 70 percent of the Philippines' total land area in 1900 to about 18.3 percent in 1999.[^cite_note-FAO-Forests-285] Rehabilitation efforts have had marginal success.[^cite_note-286]&#x20;

The Philippines is a [priority hotspot for biodiversity conservation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biodiversity_hotspot);[^cite_note-287][^cite_note-Berba-Matias-2022-261] it has [more than 200 protected areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_protected_areas_of_the_Philippines),[^cite_note-288] which was expanded to 7,790,000 hectares (30,100 sq mi) as of 2023.[^cite_note-289] [Three sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_World_Heritage_Sites_in_the_Philippines) in the Philippines have been included on the UNESCO World Heritage List: the [Tubbataha Reef](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tubbataha_Reef) in the Sulu Sea,[^cite_note-290] the [Puerto Princesa Subterranean River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puerto_Princesa_Subterranean_River_National_Park),[^cite_note-291] and the [Mount Hamiguitan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Hamiguitan) Wildlife Sanctuary.[^cite_note-292]&#x20;

### Climate

Main article: [Climate of the Philippines](18906679.md)



Evening thunderstorms bringing rain over the Philippines are common from June to November.

The Philippines has a tropical maritime climate which is usually hot and humid. There are three seasons: a hot [dry season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dry_season) from March to May, a [rainy season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wet_season) from June to November, and a cool dry season from December to February.[^cite_note-LocGov-Philippines-293] The southwest [monsoon](57630.md) (known as the *habagat*) lasts from May to October. The northeast monsoon (*[amihan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amihan)*) lasts from November to April.[^cite_note-Carating-2014-294]<sup>: [24–25](https://books.google.com/books?id=sY7EBAAAQBAJ\&pg=PA24) </sup>&#x20;

The coolest month is January, and the warmest is May. Temperatures at sea level across the Philippines tend to be in the same range, regardless of latitude. The average annual temperature is around 26.6 °C (79.9 °F). It is 18.3 °C (64.9 °F) in [Baguio](162480.md), 1,500 meters (4,900 ft) above sea level.[^cite_note-PAGASA-Climate-295] The country's average [humidity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humidity) is 82 percent.[^cite_note-Carating-2014-294]<sup>: [24–25](https://books.google.com/books?id=sY7EBAAAQBAJ\&pg=PA24) </sup> Annual rainfall is as high as 5,000 millimeters (200 in) on the mountainous east coast, but less than 1,000 millimeters (39 in) in some sheltered valleys.[^cite_note-LocGov-Philippines-293]&#x20;

The [Philippine Area of Responsibility](4272946.md) has 19 [typhoons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Typhoons_in_the_Philippines) in a typical year,[^cite_note-296] usually from July to October.[^cite_note-LocGov-Philippines-293] Eight or nine of them make [landfall](5016402.md).[^cite_note-297][^cite_note-298] The wettest recorded typhoon to hit the Philippines dropped 2,210 millimeters (87 in) in Baguio from July 14 to 18, 1911.[^cite_note-299] The country [is among](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_in_the_Philippines) the world's ten most [vulnerable to climate change](66204363.md).[^cite_note-300][^cite_note-301]&#x20;

## Government and politics

Main articles: [Government of the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_the_Philippines) and [Politics of the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_the_Philippines)

See also: [Political history of the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_history_of_the_Philippines)

Large white-and-red building on a river

[Malacañang Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malaca%C3%B1ang_Palace) is the president's official residence.



[Bongbong Marcos](572460.md)\
[President](271126.md)&#x20;



[Sara Duterte](42504861.md)\
[Vice President](2271639.md)&#x20;

The Philippines has a [democratic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democracy) government, a constitutional [republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic) with a [presidential system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_system).[^cite_note-Rose-Ackerman-302] The [president](271126.md) is [head of state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_of_state) and [head of government](37570.md),[^cite_note-Banlaoi-2009-303] and is the [commander-in-chief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commander-in-chief) of the [armed forces](23448.md).[^cite_note-Rose-Ackerman-302] The president [is elected](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_elections_in_the_Philippines) through [direct election](652563.md) by the [citizens of the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine_nationality_law) for a six-year term.[^cite_note-304] The [vice president](2271639.md), limited to two consecutive six-year terms, is elected separately from the president.[^cite_note-Lazo-305] This means the president and vice president may be from different political parties. The president appoints and presides over the [cabinet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabinet_of_the_Philippines) and officials of various national government agencies and institutions.[^cite_note-Lazo-2009-306]<sup>: [213–214](https://books.google.com/books?id=fMszAErMRKYC\&pg=213) </sup> The [bicameral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bicameralism) [Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congress_of_the_Philippines) is composed of the [Senate](299521.md) (the [upper house](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_house), with members [elected to a six-year term](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine_Senate_elections)) and the [House of Representatives](293605.md) (the [lower house](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lower_house), with members elected to a three-year term).[^cite_note-CarterCenterOrg-2010-Elections-307]&#x20;

Senators are elected [at-large](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/At-large),[^cite_note-CarterCenterOrg-2010-Elections-307] and representatives are elected from [legislative districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislative_districts_of_the_Philippines) and [party lists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Party-list_representation_in_the_House_of_Representatives_of_the_Philippines).[^cite_note-Lazo-2009-306]<sup>: [162–163](https://books.google.com/books?id=fMszAErMRKYC\&pg=162) </sup> Judicial authority is vested in the [Supreme Court](1335534.md), composed of a [chief justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Justice_of_the_Philippines) and fourteen [associate justices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Associate_Justice_of_the_Supreme_Court_of_the_Philippines),[^cite_note-308] who are appointed by the president from nominations submitted by the [Judicial and Bar Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judicial_and_Bar_Council).[^cite_note-Rose-Ackerman-302]&#x20;

[Attempts to change](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_reform_in_the_Philippines) the government to a [federal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federation), [unicameral](204304.md), or [parliamentary government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliamentary_system) have been made since the [Ramos administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Fidel_V._Ramos).[^cite_note-309] Philippine politics tends to be dominated by [well-known families](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_political_families_in_the_Philippines), such as [political dynasties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_dynasties_in_the_Philippines) or [celebrities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celebrity_influence_in_politics),[^cite_note-310][^cite_note-311] and [party switching](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Party_switching) is widely practiced.[^cite_note-312] Corruption is significant,[^cite_note-313][^cite_note-314][^cite_note-315] attributed by some historians to the Spanish colonial period's [padrino system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Padrino_system).[^cite_note-316][^cite_note-317] The Roman Catholic church exerts considerable but waning[^cite_note-318] influence in political affairs, although a constitutional provision for the [separation of Church and State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Separation_of_Church_and_State) exists.[^cite_note-319]&#x20;

### Foreign relations

Main article: [Foreign relations of the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_the_Philippines)

Color-coded world map

[Philippine diplomatic missions worldwide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_diplomatic_missions_of_the_Philippines)

A [founding and active member](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippines_and_the_United_Nations) of the United Nations,[^cite_note-Buhler-2001-148]<sup>: [37–38](https://books.google.com/books?id=Ty7NAG1Jl-8C\&pg=PA37) </sup> the Philippines has been a non-permanent member of the [Security Council](31956.md).[^cite_note-320] The country participates in [peacekeeping](84617.md) missions, particularly in [East Timor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Transitional_Administration_in_East_Timor).[^cite_note-321][^cite_note-322] The Philippines is a [founding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ASEAN_Declaration) and active member of [ASEAN](106539.md) (Association of Southeast Asian Nations)[^cite_note-323][^cite_note-324] and a member of the [East Asia Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Asia_Summit),[^cite_note-325] the [Group of 24](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Group_of_24),[^cite_note-326] and the [Non-Aligned Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Aligned_Movement).[^cite_note-327] The country has sought to obtain observer status in the [Organisation of Islamic Cooperation](47725.md) since 2003,[^cite_note-328][^cite_note-329] and was a member of [SEATO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SEATO).[^cite_note-330][^cite_note-331] Over 10 million Filipinos [live](2257094.md) and [work in 200 countries](3278907.md),[^cite_note-332][^cite_note-333] giving the Philippines [soft power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soft_power).[^cite_note-Thompson-Batalla-2018-172]<sup>: [207](https://books.google.com/books?id=DmkPEAAAQBAJ\&pg=PA207) </sup>&#x20;

During the 1990s, the Philippines began to seek [economic liberalization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_liberalization) and [free trade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_trade)[^cite_note-ILOOrg-2019-334]<sup>: 7–8 </sup> to help spur [foreign direct investment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_direct_investment).[^cite_note-335] It is a member of the [World Trade Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Organization)[^cite_note-ILOOrg-2019-334]<sup>: 8 </sup> and the [Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asia-Pacific_Economic_Cooperation).[^cite_note-336] The Philippines entered into the [ASEAN Trade in Goods Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ASEAN_Free_Trade_Area) in 2010[^cite_note-337] and the [Regional Comprehensive Economic Partnership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regional_Comprehensive_Economic_Partnership) [free trade agreement](2533562.md) (FTA) in 2023.[^cite_note-338][^cite_note-339] Through ASEAN, the Philippines has signed FTAs with [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ASEAN%E2%80%93China_Free_Trade_Area), [India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ASEAN%E2%80%93India_Free_Trade_Area), Japan, South Korea, Australia and New Zealand.[^cite_note-ILOOrg-2019-334]<sup>: 15 </sup> The country has bilateral FTAs with [Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japan%E2%80%93Philippines_Economic_Partnership_Agreement), South Korea,[^cite_note-340] and [four European states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Free_Trade_Association): Iceland, Liechtenstein, Norway and Switzerland.[^cite_note-ILOOrg-2019-334]<sup>: 9–10, 15 </sup>&#x20;



Filipino soldiers painting a U.S. and Philippine flag

The Philippines has a long [relationship with the United States](15994099.md), involving economics, [security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIA_activities_in_the_Philippines), and interpersonal relations.[^cite_note-341] The Philippines' location [serves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_bases_in_the_Philippines) an [important role](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_island_chain) in the United States' [island chain strategy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Island_chain_strategy) in the West Pacific;[^cite_note-342][^cite_note-343] a [Mutual Defense Treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mutual_Defense_Treaty_\(United_States%E2%80%93Philippines\)) between the two countries was signed in 1951, and was supplemented with the [1999 Visiting Forces Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippines%E2%80%93United_States_Visiting_Forces_Agreement) and the 2016 [Enhanced Defense Cooperation Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enhanced_Defense_Cooperation_Agreement).[^cite_note-344] The country supported American policies during the [Cold War](325329.md) and participated in the [Korean](16772.md) and [Vietnam](32611.md) wars.[^cite_note-345][^cite_note-346] In 2003, the Philippines was designated a major non-NATO ally.[^cite_note-347] [Under President Duterte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Rodrigo_Duterte), ties with the United States weakened in favor of improved relations with China and [Russia](21761997.md).[^cite_note-DeCastro-2022-348][^cite_note-349][^cite_note-350] The Philippines relies heavily on the United States for its external defense;[^cite_note-Lum-Dolven-2014-195]<sup>: 11 </sup> the U.S. has made regular assurances to defend the Philippines,[^cite_note-351] including the [South China Sea](74209.md).[^cite_note-352]&#x20;

Since 1975, the Philippines has valued its [relations with China](33734272.md)[^cite_note-353]—its top trading partner,[^cite_note-354] and cooperates significantly with the country.[^cite_note-355][^cite_note-DeCastro-2022-348] Japan is the biggest bilateral contributor of [official development assistance](1316305.md) to the Philippines;[^cite_note-356][^cite_note-357] although some tension exists because of [World War II](32927.md), much animosity has faded.[^cite_note-Dolan-1991-90]<sup>: [93](https://web.archive.org/web/20060927161330/http://countrystudies.us/philippines/93.htm) </sup> Historical and cultural ties continue to affect [relations with Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippines%E2%80%93Spain_relations).[^cite_note-358][^cite_note-359] Relations with Middle Eastern countries are shaped by the high number of Filipinos working in those countries,[^cite_note-360] and by issues related to the Muslim minority in the Philippines;[^cite_note-361] concerns have been raised about domestic abuse and war affecting the approximately 2.5 million overseas Filipino workers in the region.[^cite_note-362][^cite_note-363]&#x20;

The Philippines [has claims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spratly_Islands_dispute) in the [Spratly Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spratly_Islands) which overlap with claims by China, Malaysia, Taiwan, and Vietnam.[^cite_note-364] The largest of [its controlled islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippines_and_the_Spratly_Islands) is [Thitu Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thitu_Island), which contains the [Philippines' smallest town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalayaan,_Palawan).[^cite_note-365] The 2012 [Scarborough Shoal standoff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scarborough_Shoal_standoff), after China seized the shoal from the Philippines, led to an [international arbitration case](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippines_v._China)[^cite_note-366] which the Philippines eventually won;[^cite_note-367] China rejected the result,[^cite_note-368] and other new Philippine maritime laws,[^cite_note-369] and made the shoal a prominent symbol of the broader dispute.[^cite_note-370]&#x20;

### Military

Main article: [Armed Forces of the Philippines](23448.md)



[BRP Miguel Malvar (FFG6)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BRP_Miguel_Malvar_\(FFG-6\)) arrives at Joint Base Pearl Harbor-Hickam to participate in Exercise Rim of the Pacific (RIMPAC) 2026.

The [volunteer](9354649.md) Armed Forces of the Philippines (AFP) consist of three branches: the [Philippine Air Force](27918720.md), the [Philippine Army](6032559.md), and the [Philippine Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine_Navy).[^cite_note-371][^cite_note-372] Civilian security is handled by the [Philippine National Police](1313055.md) under the [Department of the Interior and Local Government](4718691.md).[^cite_note-373] The AFP had a total manpower of around 280,000 as of 2022, of which 130,000 were active military personnel, 100,000 were reserves, and 50,000 were [paramilitaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Citizen_Armed_Force_Geographical_Unit).[^cite_note-374]&#x20;

In 2023, US\$477 million, 1.4 percent of GDP, was spent on the Philippine military.[^cite_note-375][^cite_note-376] Most of the country's defense spending is on the Philippine Army, which leads operations against internal threats such as [communist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_armed_conflicts_in_the_Philippines) and [Muslim](4602307.md) separatist insurgencies; its preoccupation with internal security contributed to the decline of [Philippine naval capability](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_equipment_of_the_Philippine_Navy) which began during the 1970s.[^cite_note-377] [A military modernization program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFP_Modernization_Act) began in 1995[^cite_note-378] and expanded in 2012 to build a more capable defense system.[^cite_note-379]&#x20;

The Philippines has long struggled against [local insurgencies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_conflict_in_the_Philippines), [separatism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Separatism_in_the_Philippines), and [terrorism](9101671.md).[^cite_note-380][^cite_note-Croissant-2017-381][^cite_note-382] [Bangsamoro](37260549.md)'s largest separatist organizations, the [Moro National Liberation Front](2821886.md) and the [Moro Islamic Liberation Front](51673.md), signed final peace agreements with the government in [1996](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1996_Final_Peace_Agreement) and [2014](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comprehensive_Agreement_on_the_Bangsamoro) respectively.[^cite_note-383][^cite_note-384] Other, more-militant groups such as [Abu Sayyaf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Sayyaf) and [Bangsamoro Islamic Freedom Fighters](39963003.md)[^cite_note-CTCSentinel-BIFF-385] have kidnapped foreigners for ransom, particularly in the Sulu Archipelago[^cite_note-386][^cite_note-387] and [Maguindanao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maguindanao),[^cite_note-CTCSentinel-BIFF-385] but their presence has been reduced.[^cite_note-388][^cite_note-389]&#x20;

The [Communist Party of the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_Party_of_the_Philippines) (CPP) and its military wing, the [New People's Army](1262096.md) (NPA), have been waging [guerrilla warfare](18748169.md) against the government since the 1970s and have [engaged in ambushes, bombings, and assassinations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_communist_rebellion_in_the_Philippines) of government officials and security forces;[^cite_note-CIAWorldFactBook-2022-2023-390] although shrinking militarily and politically after the return of democracy in 1986,[^cite_note-Croissant-2017-381][^cite_note-391] the CPP-NPA, through the [National Democratic Front of the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Democratic_Front_of_the_Philippines), continues to gather public support in urban areas by setting up [communist fronts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_front), infiltrating sectoral organizations, and rallying public discontent and increased militancy against the government.[^cite_note-392] The Philippines ranked 104th out of 163 countries in the 2024 [Global Peace Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Peace_Index).[^cite_note-393]&#x20;

### Administrative divisions

Main article: [Administrative divisions of the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_the_Philippines)

Color-coded political map of the Philippines

The Philippines' regions and provinces

The Philippines is divided into 18 [regions](216786.md), 82 [provinces](165100.md), 149 [cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cities_of_the_Philippines), 1,493 [municipalities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipalities_of_the_Philippines), and 42,011 [barangays](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barangay).[^cite_note-394] Regions other than [Bangsamoro](37260549.md) are divided for administrative convenience.[^cite_note-395] [Calabarzon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calabarzon) was the region with the greatest population as of 2020. The [National Capital Region](72340.md) (NCR) was the most densely populated.[^cite_note-PSA-2020-Census-Highlights-396]&#x20;

The Philippines is a [unitary state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unitary_state), with the system being in place ever since the [Spanish colonialization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Captaincy_General_of_the_Philippines), with the exception of the [Bangsamoro Autonomous Region in Muslim Mindanao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangsamoro_Autonomous_Region_in_Muslim_Mindanao) (BARMM).[^cite_note-397][^cite_note-Unitary-398] There have been [steps towards decentralization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federalism_in_the_Philippines).[^cite_note-399][^cite_note-400] A 1991 law [devolved](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Devolution) some powers to [local governments](22088280.md).[^cite_note-401]&#x20;

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of the Philippines](23445.md)

According to the [International Monetary Fund](15251.md) (IMF) in its October 2025 update, the Philippines is the world's 33rd largest economy with a nominal [gross domestic product](12594.md) (GDP) of \$494.16 billion.[^cite_note-IMF_WEO_Oct2025-402] As a [newly industrialized country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newly_industrialized_country),[^cite_note-FederalRegister-Vol78-403][^cite_note-404] the Philippine economy has been transitioning from an agricultural base to one with more emphasis on services and manufacturing.[^cite_note-FederalRegister-Vol78-403][^cite_note-405] The country's labor force is about 52.09 million[^cite_note-ph-employmentrate-406] with an [unemployment rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_sovereign_states_by_unemployment_rate) of 5.1 percent as of February 2026.[^cite_note-ph-employmentrate-406] Gross international reserves totalled US\$106.64 billion as of March 2026.[^cite_note-407] [Debt-to-GDP ratio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Debt-to-GDP_ratio) decreased to 60.2 percent at the end of 2023 from a 17-year high 63.7 percent at the end of the third quarter of that year, and indicated resiliency during the [COVID-19 pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_the_Philippines).[^cite_note-408] The country's unit of [currency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Currency) is the [Philippine peso](227672.md) (₱[^cite_note-409] or PHP[^cite_note-410]).[^cite_note-411]&#x20;
