The Philippines is a net importer,[^cite_note-ILOOrg-2019-334]<sup>: 55–56, 61–65, 77, 83, 111 </sup>[^cite_note-412] and a [debtor nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Net_international_investment_position).[^cite_note-413] As of 2020, the country's main export markets were China, the United States, Japan, Hong Kong, and Singapore;[^cite_note-OECWorld-PH-414] primary exports included [integrated circuits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Integrated_circuit), office machinery and parts, electrical transformers, insulated wiring, and [semiconductors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Semiconductor).[^cite_note-OECWorld-PH-414] Its primary import markets that year were China, Japan, South Korea, the United States, and Indonesia.[^cite_note-OECWorld-PH-414] Major export crops include [coconuts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coconut_production_in_the_Philippines), [bananas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banana_industry#Production_and_export), and [pineapples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_pineapple_production); it is the world's largest producer of [abaca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abaca),[^cite_note-Boquet-2017-225]<sup>: [226–242](https://books.google.com/books?id=90C4DgAAQBAJ\&pg=PA226) </sup> and was the world's second biggest exporter of [nickel ore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nickel) in 2022,[^cite_note-415] as well as the biggest exporter of gold-clad metals and the biggest importer of [copra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copra) in 2020.[^cite_note-OECWorld-PH-414]&#x20;

Modern, landscaped office building

Headquarters of the [International Rice Research Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Rice_Research_Institute) in [Los Baños, Laguna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Ba%C3%B1os,_Laguna)

With an average annual growth rate of six to seven percent since around 2010, the Philippines has emerged as one of the world's fastest-growing economies,[^cite_note-416] driven primarily by its increasing reliance on the service sector.[^cite_note-OECD-SMEPolicyIndex2018-417] Regional development is uneven, however, with Manila (in particular) gaining most of the new economic growth.[^cite_note-418][^cite_note-419] [Remittances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Remittance) from [overseas Filipinos](2257094.md), mainly [seamen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sailor)[^cite_note-420] and [nurses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nurses),[^cite_note-421] which the country is the world's largest suppliers of, contribute significantly to the country's economy;[^cite_note-422][^cite_note-OECD-SMEPolicyIndex2018-417] they reached a record US\$37.20 billion in 2023, accounting for 8.5 percent of GDP.[^cite_note-423] The Philippines is the world's primary [business process outsourcing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Business_process_outsourcing_in_the_Philippines) (BPO) center.[^cite_note-424][^cite_note-425] About 1.3 million Filipinos work in the BPO sector, primarily in [customer service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Customer_service).[^cite_note-426] The country is a pioneer in digital finance, as the first country to put its entire national budget under the [blockchain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blockchain) system.[^cite_note-427]&#x20;

Like many other governments, the Philippine government engages in [for profit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/For_profit) activities through [state-owned enterprises](204768.md) known as [Government-Owned and Controlled Corporations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government-owned_and_controlled_corporation) (GOCCs). With the exception of the [Bangko Sentral ng Pilipinas](1276593.md), all GOCCs (including the controversial new [Maharlika Investment Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharlika_Investment_Corporation)) fall under the supervision of the [Governance Commission for GOCCs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governance_Commission_for_GOCCs) (GCG).[^cite_note-428]&#x20;

### Science and technology

Main article: [Science and technology in the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_and_technology_in_the_Philippines)

Further information: [Philippine space program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine_space_program)

The Philippines has one of the largest agricultural-research systems in Asia, despite relatively low spending on agricultural research and development.[^cite_note-429][^cite_note-430] The country has developed new varieties of crops, including [rice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rice_production_in_the_Philippines),[^cite_note-431][^cite_note-432] coconuts,[^cite_note-433] and bananas.[^cite_note-434] Research organizations include the [Philippine Rice Research Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine_Rice_Research_Institute)[^cite_note-435] and the [International Rice Research Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Rice_Research_Institute).[^cite_note-436]&#x20;

The [Philippine Space Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine_Space_Agency) maintains the [country's space program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine_space_program),[^cite_note-437][^cite_note-438] and the country bought its first [satellite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Philippine_satellites) in 1996.[^cite_note-439] [Diwata-1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diwata-1), its first [micro-satellite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Small_satellite#Microsatellites), was launched on the United States' [Cygnus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cygnus_\(spacecraft\)) spacecraft in 2016.[^cite_note-440]&#x20;

The Philippines has a [high concentration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_smartphone_penetration) of [cellular-phone](19644137.md) users,[^cite_note-441] and a high level of [mobile commerce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mobile_commerce).[^cite_note-442] [Text messaging](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Text_messaging) is a popular form of communication, and the nation sent an average of one billion [SMS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SMS) messages per day in 2007.[^cite_note-443] The [Philippine telecommunications industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telecommunications_in_the_Philippines) had been dominated by the [PLDT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PLDT)-[Globe Telecom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Globe_Telecom) duopoly for more than two decades,[^cite_note-444] and the 2021 entry of [Dito Telecommunity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dito_Telecommunity) improved the country's telecommunications service.[^cite_note-445] The Philippines was ranked 50th in the [Global Innovation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index) in 2025.[^cite_note-446][^cite_note-447]&#x20;

### Tourism

Main article: [Tourism in the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_the_Philippines)

People on an observation deck overlooking hills

Tourists at [Chocolate Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chocolate_Hills), conical [karst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karst) hills in [Bohol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bohol)

The Philippines is a popular retirement destination for foreigners because of its climate and low cost of living.[^cite_note-448] The country's main tourist attractions are [its numerous beaches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_beaches_in_the_Philippines);[^cite_note-Woods-2006-66]<sup>: [109](https://books.google.com/books?id=eajOEAAAQBAJ\&pg=109) </sup>[^cite_note-449] the Philippines is also a top destination for diving enthusiasts.[^cite_note-450][^cite_note-451] [Tourist spots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tourist_attractions_in_the_Philippines) include [Boracay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boracay), called the best island in the world by *[Travel + Leisure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Travel_%2B_Leisure)* in 2012;[^cite_note-452] [Coron](498917.md) and [El Nido](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Nido,_Palawan) in Palawan; [Cebu](160278.md); [Siargao](2855427.md), and [Bohol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bohol).[^cite_note-453]&#x20;

Tourism contributed 5.2 percent to the Philippine GDP in 2021 (lower than 12.7 percent in 2019, before the COVID-19 pandemic),[^cite_note-454] and provided 5.7 million jobs in 2019.[^cite_note-455] The Philippines attracted 5.45 million international visitors in 2023, 30 percent lower than the 8.26 million record in pre-pandemic 2019; most tourists came from South Korea (26.4 percent), United States (16.5 percent), Japan (5.6 percent), Australia (4.89 percent), and China (4.84 percent).[^cite_note-456]&#x20;

## Infrastructure

### Transportation

Main article: [Transportation in the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transportation_in_the_Philippines)

Two white buses side by side, one larger than the other

Traditional *(left)* and modern [jeepneys](884965.md) in [Quezon City](284223.md). Public utility vehicles older than 15 years are [gradually being phased out](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_Utility_Vehicle_Modernization_Program) in favor of eco-friendly [Euro 4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_emission_standards)-compliant vehicles.

[^cite_note-457]

Transportation in the Philippines is by road, air, rail and water. Roads are the dominant form of transport, carrying 98 percent of people and 58 percent of cargo.[^cite_note-ADBOrg-PH-Transport-458] In December 2018, there were 210,528 kilometers (130,816 mi) of roads in the country.[^cite_note-459] The backbone of land-based transportation in the country is the [Pan-Philippine Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pan-Philippine_Highway), which connects the islands of Luzon, Samar, Leyte, and Mindanao.[^cite_note-460] Inter-island transport is by the 919-kilometer (571 mi) [Strong Republic Nautical Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strong_Republic_Nautical_Highway), an integrated set of highways and [ferry](50771.md) routes linking 17 cities.[^cite_note-461][^cite_note-462] [Jeepneys](884965.md) are a popular, iconic public utility vehicle;[^cite_note-Boquet-2017-225]<sup>: [496–497](https://books.google.com/books?id=90C4DgAAQBAJ\&pg=PA496) </sup> other public land transport includes [buses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_bus_companies_of_the_Philippines), [UV Express](40666464.md), TNVS, Filcab, [taxis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taxis_of_the_Philippines), and [tricycles](29843385.md).[^cite_note-463][^cite_note-464] Traffic is a significant issue [in Manila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traffic_in_Metro_Manila) and on arterial roads to the capital.[^cite_note-465][^cite_note-466]&#x20;

Despite wider historical use,[^cite_note-467] [rail transportation in the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_transportation_in_the_Philippines) is limited[^cite_note-Boquet-2017-225]<sup>: [491](https://books.google.com/books?id=90C4DgAAQBAJ\&pg=PA491) </sup> to [transporting passengers within Metro Manila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_transportation_in_the_Greater_Manila_Area) and the provinces of [Laguna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laguna_\(province\))[^cite_note-468] and [Quezon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quezon),[^cite_note-469] with a short track in the [Bicol Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bicol_Region).[^cite_note-Boquet-2017-225]<sup>: [491](https://books.google.com/books?id=90C4DgAAQBAJ\&pg=PA491) </sup> The country had a railway footprint of only 79 kilometers (49 mi) as of 2019, which it planned to expand to 244 kilometers (152 mi).[^cite_note-470] A revival of [freight rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freight_rail) is planned to reduce road congestion.[^cite_note-471][^cite_note-472]&#x20;

The Philippines had [90 national government-owned airports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_airports_in_the_Philippines) as of 2022, of which eight are [international](1519705.md).[^cite_note-PIDSGovPH-Francisco-Lim-473] [Ninoy Aquino International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ninoy_Aquino_International_Airport), formerly known as Manila International Airport, has the [greatest number of passengers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_the_busiest_airports_in_the_Philippines).[^cite_note-PIDSGovPH-Francisco-Lim-473] The 2017 [air domestic market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_airlines_of_the_Philippines) was dominated by [Philippine Airlines](296889.md), the country's [flag carrier](465816.md) and Asia's oldest commercial airline,[^cite_note-474][^cite_note-475] and [Cebu Pacific](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cebu_Pacific) (the country's leading [low-cost carrier](428422.md)).[^cite_note-476][^cite_note-477]&#x20;

A variety of boats are used throughout the Philippines;[^cite_note-478] most are [double-outrigger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Outrigger_boat) vessels known as *banca*[^cite_note-Aguilar-2004-479] or *[bangka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangka_\(boat\))*.[^cite_note-Funtecha-2000-480] Modern ships use [plywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plywood) instead of logs, and motor engines instead of sails;[^cite_note-Aguilar-2004-479] they are used for fishing and inter-island travel.[^cite_note-Funtecha-2000-480] The Philippines has [over 1,800 seaports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ports_in_the_Philippines);[^cite_note-481] of these, the principal seaports of [Manila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Manila) (the country's chief, and busiest, port),[^cite_note-482] [Batangas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Batangas_International_Port), [Subic Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Subic_Bay), [Cebu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Cebu), [Iloilo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Iloilo), [Davao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Davao), [Cagayan de Oro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Cagayan_de_Oro), [General Santos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_General_Santos), and [Zamboanga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Zamboanga) are part of the ASEAN Transport Network.[^cite_note-483][^cite_note-484]&#x20;

### Energy

Main article: [Energy in the Philippines](40257579.md)

A large dam, seen from above

The [Ambuklao Dam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ambuklao_Dam) on the [Agno River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agno_River) in [Bokod, Benguet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bokod,_Benguet)

The Philippines had a total installed power capacity of 26,882 [MW](21347693.md) in 2021; 43 percent was generated from [coal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coal_mining_in_the_Philippines), 14 percent from [oil](4207510.md), 14 percent [hydropower](14073.md), 12 percent from [natural gas](22131.md), and seven percent from [geothermal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geothermal_power) sources.[^cite_note-485] It is the world's third-biggest [geothermal-energy producer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geothermal_power_in_the_Philippines), behind the United States and Indonesia.[^cite_note-486] The country's largest dam is the 1.2-kilometer-long (0.75 mi) [San Roque Dam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Roque_Dam_\(Philippines\)) on the [Agno River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agno_River) in [Pangasinan](1691800.md).[^cite_note-487] The [Malampaya gas field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malampaya_gas_field), discovered in the early 1990s off the coast of Palawan, reduced the Philippines' reliance on imported oil; it provides about 40 percent of Luzon's energy requirements, and 30 percent of the country's energy needs.[^cite_note-Boquet-2017-225]<sup>: [347](https://books.google.com/books?id=90C4DgAAQBAJ\&pg=PA347) </sup>[^cite_note-488]&#x20;

The Philippines has three [electrical grids](20344155.md), one each for Luzon, Visayas, and Mindanao.[^cite_note-Ma-2016-489] The [National Grid Corporation of the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Grid_Corporation_of_the_Philippines) manages [the country's power grid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electricity_sector_in_the_Philippines) since 2009[^cite_note-490] and provides [overhead transmission lines](1355057.md) across the country's islands. Electric distribution to consumers [is provided](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_electric_distribution_utilities_in_the_Philippines) by privately owned distribution utilities and government-owned [electric cooperatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_cooperative).[^cite_note-Ma-2016-489] As of end-2021, the Philippines' household electrification level was about 95.41%.[^cite_note-491]&#x20;

Plans to harness [nuclear energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_power_in_the_Philippines) began during the early 1970s during the [presidency of Ferdinand Marcos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Ferdinand_Marcos) in response to the [1973 oil crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1973_oil_crisis).[^cite_note-WorldNuclearOrg-Philippines-492] The Philippines completed [Southeast Asia's first nuclear power plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bataan_Nuclear_Power_Plant) in [Bataan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bataan) in 1984.[^cite_note-Pekkanen-2021-493] Political issues following Marcos' ouster and safety concerns after the 1986 [Chernobyl disaster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chernobyl_disaster) prevented the plant from being commissioned,[^cite_note-494][^cite_note-WorldNuclearOrg-Philippines-492] and plans to operate it remain controversial.[^cite_note-Pekkanen-2021-493][^cite_note-495]&#x20;

### Water supply and sanitation

Main article: [Water supply and sanitation in the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_supply_and_sanitation_in_the_Philippines)

A low, blue building

A water-district office in [Banate, Iloilo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banate,_Iloilo)

Water supply and sanitation outside Metro Manila is provided by the government through local [water districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_district) in cities or towns.[^cite_note-496][^cite_note-Kohsaka-2007-497][^cite_note-498] Metro Manila is served by [Manila Water](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manila_Water) and [Maynilad Water Services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maynilad_Water_Services). Except for shallow wells for domestic use, groundwater users are required to obtain a permit from the [National Water Resources Board](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Water_Resources_Board).[^cite_note-Kohsaka-2007-497] In 2022, the total water withdrawals increased to 91 billion cubic meters (3.2×1&#x30;~~^~~<sup>12</sup> cu ft) from 89 billion cubic meters (3.1×1&#x30;~~^~~<sup>12</sup> cu ft) in 2021 and the total expenditures on water were amounted to [₱](227672.md)144.81 billion.[^cite_note-499]&#x20;

Most sewage in the Philippines flows into septic tanks.[^cite_note-Kohsaka-2007-497] In 2015, the [Joint Monitoring Programme for Water Supply and Sanitation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Monitoring_Programme_for_Water_Supply_and_Sanitation) noted that 74 percent of the Philippine population had access to [improved sanitation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Improved_sanitation) and "good progress" had been made between 1990 and 2015.[^cite_note-500] 96 percent of Filipino households had an improved source of drinking water and 92 percent of households had sanitary toilet facilities as of 2016; connections of toilet facilities to appropriate sewerage systems remain largely insufficient, however, especially in rural and urban poor communities.[^cite_note-DOH-2018-501]<sup>: 46 </sup>&#x20;

## Demographics

Main article: [Demographics of the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_the_Philippines)

See also: [List of cities in the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_in_the_Philippines)

In July 2024, the Philippines had a population of 112,729,484.[^cite_note-PSA_POPCEN-17] More than 60 percent of the country's population live in the [coastal zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coastal_zone).[^cite_note-502] In 2020, 54 percent lived in [urban areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_area).[^cite_note-PSAGovPH-2020-Urban-503] [Manila](184334.md), its capital, and [Quezon City](284223.md), the country's most populous city, are in [Metro Manila](72340.md). About 13.48 million people, 12 percent of the Philippines' population, live in Metro Manila,[^cite_note-PSAGovPH-2020-Urban-503] the [country's most populous metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_metropolitan_areas_in_the_Philippines)[^cite_note-504] and the world's [fifth most populous](14649921.md).[^cite_note-505] Between 1948 and 2010, the population of the Philippines increased almost fivefold, from 19 million to 92 million.[^cite_note-506]&#x20;

The [country's median age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_median_age) is 25.3, and 63.9 percent of its population is between 15 and 64 years old.[^cite_note-507] The Philippines' average annual population growth rate is decreasing,[^cite_note-508] although government attempts to further reduce population growth [have been contentious](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reproductive_Health_Bill_\(Philippines\)).[^cite_note-509] The country [reduced its poverty rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poverty_in_the_Philippines) from 49.2 percent in 1985,[^cite_note-WorldBank-Poverty-2023-510] to 18.1 percent in 2021.[^cite_note-511] [The Philippines' income inequality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Income_inequality_in_the_Philippines) began to decline in 2012.[^cite_note-WorldBank-Poverty-2023-510]&#x20;
